   - Path 10: N -> B -> G -> H -> J -> K -> X -> F -> M -> C -> O -> I -> P

   - Node G: Appears in all paths.
   - Either B or G must be removed, but removing both is redundant since both connect to the same subgraphs.

   - Removing B alone will disconnect all paths because all paths pass through B.
   - No smaller set of nodes can disconnect N and P because B is a critical hub.

Image name: FD-K_task2_gt2_PAIR1
Task: task2_min_nodes_removal
Model: mistral-small-3.1-24b
Mode: few_shot, COT: cot, Attempt: 2
--- Model Response ---
To solve this problem, I need to find the minimum number of nodes that must be removed to completely disconnect the two black nodes, M and N, without removing them. Let's examine the paths between M and N and determine the nodes that need to be removed.

### Step-by-Step Reasoning:

1. **Identify the Target Nodes:**
   - The target nodes are M and N.

2. **Identify All Paths Between M and N:**
   - **Path 1:** M -> E -> H -> B -> C -> N
   - **Path 2:** M -> E -> H -> B -> I -> C -> N
   - **Path 3:** M -> E -> H -> B -> I -> J -> P -> O -> N
   - **Path 4:** M -> L -> E -> H -> B -> C -> N
   - **Path 5:** M -> L -> E -> H -> B -> I -> C -> N
   - **Path 6:** M -> L -> E -> H -> B -> I -> J -> P -> O -> N

3. **Determine Critical Nodes:**
   - **Node E:** Removing E breaks Paths 1, 2, 3, 4, and 5.
   - **Node H:** Removing H breaks Paths 1, 2, 3, 4, and 5.
   - **Node B:** Removing B breaks Paths 1, 2, 3, 4, and 5.
   - **Node C:** Removing C breaks Paths 1, 2, and 5.
   - **Node I:** Removing I breaks Paths 2, 3, and 5.
   - **Node J:** Removing J breaks Path 3.
   - **Node P:** Removing P breaks Paths 3 and 6.
   - **Node O:** Removing O breaks Paths 3 and 6.

4. **Determine the Minimum Cut Set:**
   - Removing **E** or **H** or **B** will break most of the paths between M and N. However, removing any one of these nodes alone will not be sufficient because there are alternative paths.
   - For example, removing **E** will still leave Path 6 intact (M -> L -> H -> B -> I -> J -> P -> O -> N).
   - To completely disconnect M and N, we need to remove nodes from at least two independent sets of paths.
   - Removing **E** and **P** will break all paths between M and N.

### Conclusion:

The minimum number of nodes that must be removed to disconnect M and N is 2. The cut set can be [E, P].

Final Answer: Nodes to remove: 2, Cut Set: [E, P].

  - From N: Is node N connected to node O? **Yes.**
  - From Y: No connections leading to the target node J.
  - From K: Is node K connected to node J? **Yes.**

We have found a path:
- F → G → K → J

     - The paths from L to J converge at nodes E, G, H, I, and P.
   - It's observed that node E and node I are connecting points for multiple paths. Removing either of these nodes would not entirely disconnect L and J because alternative paths still exist. For instance, removing E alone would leave pathways through G and H as viable options for connections.

     - Removing E breaks Path 1, 2, 3, and 4.

4. **Verify Minimum Cut Set:**
   - Removing both E and I blocks all combinations of the aforementioned paths between L and J.
   - Additional verification confirms that removing any fewer than two nodes will leave at least one path intact.

### Conclusion:
The minimal set of nodes to remove to ensure there is no path between L and J is:

**Nodes to remove: 2, Cut Set: [E, I]**

   - **Path 5:** N -> B -> F -> X -> K -> H -> G

**To disconnect N and G completely, the minimum necessary edges to remove are critical points that block all paths:**
1. **Remove (H, G)** - This will block the direct path from N to G.
2. **Remove (B, H)** - This will block the paths through B to H.
3. **Remove (F, K)** - Our secondary paths is over here to connect G to N.

**Cut Set Verification:**
- **After Removing (H, G):** The direct path N -> B -> H -> G is blocked.
- **After Removing (B, H):** The path N -> B -> H -> G and N -> B -> F -> K -> H -> G is blocked.
- **After Removing (F, K):**N -> B -> F -> K -> H -> G is blocked.

2. **Find the edges that, when removed, will disconnect all paths between B and M.**
3. **Ensure that the selected edges form the minimal cut set.**

### Step-by-Step Reasoning:

1. **Identify all paths between B and M:**

    - **Direct Paths:**
      - B → E → M

    - **Indirect Paths:**
      - B → E → P → I → H → G → F → K → X → D → J → M
      - B → E → P → J → D → X → K → G → H → I → C → N → O → Y
      - B → O → C → N → H → I → P → E → L
      - B → E → P → J → M (This is the direct path again).

2. **Find the edges that, when removed, will disconnect all paths between B and M:**

    - **Paths through E:**
      - Removing the edge (E, M) will cut the direct path B → E → M.
      - Removing the edge (E, P) will eliminate all paths from B to E towards M via P.

    - **Paths through P:**
      - P is a common node in multiple paths. Removing (P, I) will break paths involving I.
      - Removing (P, J) will break paths involving J.

    - **Identifying critical edges (Minimal Cut):**
      - Removing (E, M) should be necessary as it directly connects B and M.
      - Removing (E, P) and (P, J) together will ensure that there is no longer another indirect path that connects B to M.

3. **Check if the selected edges form the minimal cut set:**

    - Removing (E, M), (E, P), and (P, J): Each of these removals is necessary to break all possible paths.
    - This leads to a minimal cut set since it affects all paths without leaving any path between B and M.

### Final Answer:

After analyzing all possible paths and the necessity of each edge removal, the minimal number of edges to remove is:

**Edges to remove: 3, Cut Set: [(E, M), (E, P), (P, J)]**
